) we have

\displaystyle\|J^{s}[\eqref{PU2}+\eqref{PU3}+\eqref{PU4}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{4}+}R^{2}\big\{\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}+R\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\big\}.

For (
3.71
), (
3.72
), and (
3.73
), we need to split each of these terms into the three parts as in (
3.61
). Note though that due to the signs, we can always place a projection
\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}
onto the first factor which is the smoother allowing us to gain negative powers of
M
in the estimates.
For the first two such parts, we argue as we did in (
3.43
) with (
3.75
). Here, we obtain the bound by

\displaystyle M^{-\theta}R^{2}(1+R^{2})\{\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}+R\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\big\},

for some
\theta>0
.
We then need to control the corresponding third part in (
3.61
), which we do by modifying the argument we used for (
3.63
). This gives the bound

\displaystyle M^{-\theta}(1+R^{2})\big[\big\{\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}+\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\}\|w_{2}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|W\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}\big].

Therefore, for
\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}U
, we have obtained

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{>M}U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}V\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}}\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}+(1+M^{-\theta}R)^{3}\Big[\|V\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|W\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+M^{-\theta}(R+\|v_{2}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|w_{2}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}})\big\{\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}+\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\big\}\Big]

Now we consider the low frequencies
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}U
for which we take the difference of the Duhamel formulas and have the following analogue of (
3.65
):

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\|U(0)\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}M^{1+s-s_{0}}\|G(u_{1},w_{1})-G(u_{2},w_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle+CT^{\delta}M^{\sigma+s-s_{0}}\|B(u_{1},w_{1})-B(u_{2},w_{2})\|_{X^{s_{0}-\sigma,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}_{T}}

\displaystyle+CTM(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}})R^{2}\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}.

By Lemma
3.8
, we have

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle B(u_{1},w_{1})-B(u_{2},w_{2})\|_{X^{s-\sigma,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|W\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}}\big\{\|J^{\frac{1}{4}+}u_{1}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{2}\big\}

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+\|w_{2}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}}\big\{\|J^{\frac{1}{4}+}(u_{1}+u_{2})\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|J^{\frac{1}{4}+}U\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}R\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}\big\}.

Lastly, from (
3.53
), we get

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle G(u_{1},w_{1})-G(u_{2},w_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|W\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}}\Big[\max_{j=1,2}\big(\|J^{s_{0}}u_{j}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}^{2})+R^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+(\max_{j=1,2}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}^{2}+R^{2})\big[\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}U\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}})\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+\max_{j=1,2}(R\|u_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}})\big[\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}U\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\big].

Putting all of these terms together finishes the proof of (
3.66
).
∎

4.
Trilinear estimates

We now state the crucial trilinear estimates.

Proposition 4.1

(Trilinear estimates for CCM (
1.8
))
.

Let
s\geq s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}
and
0<T\leq 1
. Then, for any
u_{j}\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{+}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s_{0},4}_{x}\cap X_{T}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}\cap X_{T}^{s_{0}-1,1}
,
j=2,3
, with

\displaystyle Y(u_{2},u_{3}):=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=2}^{3}\big(\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u_{j}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+\|u_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\big)

\displaystyle+\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{2,3\}\\
j_{1}\neq j_{2}\end{subarray}}\|u_{j_{1}}\|_{X^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}\big(\|u_{j_{2}}\|_{X^{s_{0}-1,1}_{T}}+\|u_{j_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\big)

(4.1)

and for any
v\in X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[v\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}Y(u_{2},u_{3}).

(4.2)

Proof.

By passing from
-\frac{1}{2}+\delta
to
-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta
in the modulation variable, we may gain the factor of
T^{\delta}
appearing on the right-hand side of (
4.2
). Moreover, we consider arbitrary extensions of the functions
v
and
u_{2},u_{3}
on
[0,T]
and take an infimum over all extensions at the end of the estimates to recover the time localised norms appearing in (
4.1
) and (
4.2
). Moreover, we either associate the sharp cutoff function
\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}
with the dual function (when we use duality) or with
v
(just for the bound for (
4.13
)). We omit these details to not overburden the notation.

We perform a dyadic decomposition in space:

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[v\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N>1}N^{2s}\bigg(\sum_{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{23}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}\bigg)^{2}.

(4.3)

The projections
\mathbf{P}_{+}
and
\mathbf{P}_{-}
here imply that:

\displaystyle N_{1}\gtrsim N_{23}\vee N.

(4.4)

In particular,
N_{1}\gtrsim 2
.
Thus, we control (
4.3
) under the additional restriction (
4.4
). We let

\displaystyle N_{\max}:=\max(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{23},N)

and note that

\displaystyle N_{23}\lesssim N_{2}\vee N_{3}.

(4.5)

Case 0:

N_{23}\lesssim 1
.

By duality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}\\
&=\sup_{\|g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt.\end{split}

(4.6)

For fixed
g\in X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}
with
\|g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\leq 1
, we use Hölder’s inequality and (
3.6
) to bound the integrals on the right-hand side of (
4.6
) by

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-s}(N_{2}N_{3})^{-s_{0}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}.

If
N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gtrsim N_{1}
, then we use
N_{1}^{-s}
to control the norm derivative
N^{s}
and we have a negative power of the maximum frequency to perform all of the dyadic summations. If instead
N_{2}\vee N_{3}\ll N_{1}
, then
N\sim N_{1}
. Since
s_{0}>0
, we can sum over
(N_{2},N_{3})
. As for the sum over
(N,N_{1})
, we have by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\sum_{N}N^{2s}\bigg(\sum_{N_{1}\sim N}N_{1}^{-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N}N^{2s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}^{2}\bigg(\sum_{N_{1}\sim N}N_{1}^{-2s}\bigg)\|v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}^{2}\|v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}^{2}\|v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

For the rest of the proof we assume that
N_{23}\gg 1
.

Case 1:

N_{2}\vee N_{3}\sim N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
.

We use (
4.6
).
By Hölder’s inequality and (
3.8
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt\bigg|\\
&\lesssim N_{23}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}[\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v]\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}}{N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{s_{0}}N_{3}^{s_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

(4.7)

Now, considering the dyadic factors and using (
4.4
) and (
4.5
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}}{N_{1}^{s}(N_{2}N_{3})^{s_{0}}}.

(4.8)

In this case,
N_{2}\sim N_{3}
and with (
4.4
), we must have either (i)
N_{1}\sim N
or (ii)
N\ll N_{1}
. In case (i), by (
4.5
) we have

\displaystyle\eqref{L4mult}\lesssim N_{2}^{-2s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}

which is a negative power provided that
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}+5\delta
,
and we can sum over
(N,N_{1})
using a Cauchy-Schwarz argument as in Case 0.

In case (ii), since
N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{23}\lesssim N_{2}\sim N_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{L4mult}\lesssim\frac{N^{s}}{N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{2s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}-10\delta}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-2s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}

which allows us to perform all of the dyadic sums since
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}+5\delta
.

Case 2:

N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gg N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
and
N_{2}\wedge N_{3}\gtrsim N
.

In this case, we can also proceed by using duality, (
3.8
) and (
4.7
), and we are reduced to adequately controlling the multiplicative factor (
4.8
). Note that we must have
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\vee N_{3}\sim N_{23}\sim N_{\max}
.
By (
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\eqref{L4mult}\lesssim N^{s-s_{0}}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}N_{1}^{-s-s_{0}}\lesssim N^{s-s_{0}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta-s-s_{0}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-2s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}+10\delta},

where in the second (and third) inequality we used that
s\geq s_{0}
and
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}+5\delta
.
We can then perform all of the dyadic summations since
\delta>0
.

Case 3:

N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gg N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
and
N_{2}\wedge N_{3}\ll N
.

In the remaining cases, we need to make use of the phase function.
Given
\tau,\tau_{j},\xi,\xi_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
, with
\sigma:=\tau-\xi^{2}
and
\sigma_{j}:=\tau_{j}-\xi_{j}^{2}
,
j=1,2,3
, satisfying
\tau=\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\tau_{3}
and
\xi=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}
, we have the following resonance identity

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}-\sigma=\xi_{1}^{2}-\xi_{2}^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2}-\xi^{2}=-2(\xi-\xi_{1})(\xi-\xi_{3})=:\Phi(\overline{\xi}).

(4.9)

Note that under
\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}=\xi
, we have
\xi-\xi_{1}=\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}
.
Thus, for
|\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}|\sim N_{23}\gg 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}:=\max(|\sigma_{1}|,|\sigma_{2}|,|\sigma_{3}|,|\sigma|)\gtrsim N_{23}|\xi-\xi_{3}|.

(4.10)

Moreover, from the frequency assumptions, it holds that

\displaystyle N_{23}\sim N_{2}\vee N_{3}\quad\text{and}\quad N_{1}\sim N_{\max}.

(4.11)

For notational purposes, we define

\displaystyle K:=N_{23}(N\vee N_{3}).

(4.12)

We also write
N_{(2)}=N_{2}\vee N_{3}
and
N_{(3)}=N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
, and we extend the
(j)
notation to denote the corresponding index on the functions
\{u_{2},u_{3}\}
and set
\iota_{(j)}
to be define the conjugation operation if
(j)=2
, or to be the identity operation if
(j)=3
.

For fixed
(N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{23})
, the triangle inequality implies

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\,\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

(4.13)

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}v)\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\,\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

(4.14)

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}v)\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}([\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}]^{\iota_{(3)}}\,[\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}u_{(2)}]^{\iota_{(2)}})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

(4.15)

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}v)\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}([\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}]^{\iota_{(3)}}\,[\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}u_{(2)}]^{\iota_{(2)}}]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

(4.16)

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}v)\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\,\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}},

(4.17)

where
\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}
are the projections in (
2.4
).

The terms (
4.13
) through (
4.16
) are the non-resonant contributions while (
4.17
) is the nearly-resonant contribution. As
\mathbf{P}_{+}u=u
for (
1.8
), we must have that
N_{2}\lesssim N_{3}
. However, we choose to proceed without this extra information since it will not be the case for (
2.16
).

We first control the non-resonant contributions.

Bound for (
4.13
):

By (
4.10
), (
3.6
), and Bernstein’s inequality,

(
4.13
)

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}[\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}]\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}[\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}]\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}N_{23}N_{1}^{-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}-\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N_{23}(N_{2}\wedge N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}N_{1}^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{s_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}u_{(2)}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.

We reduce to bounding the ensuing multiplier:

\displaystyle\frac{N^{s}N_{23}(N_{2}\wedge N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}N_{1}^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}(N_{2}\wedge N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{(N\vee N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}N_{1}^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{s_{0}}}.

(4.18)

If
N\sim N_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\eqref{X0mult}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}+2\delta-s_{0}+(\frac{1}{2}-s_{0})\lor 0}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-s_{0}+4\delta+(\frac{1}{2}-s_{0})\lor 0}

which is a negative power provided
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}+2\delta
.
Then, if
N\ll N_{1}
, by (
4.4
), we must have
N_{1}\sim(N_{2}\vee N_{3})
. If
N_{3}\gg N_{2}
, then,

\displaystyle\eqref{X0mult}\lesssim\frac{N^{s}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{N_{\max}^{s+s_{0}-4\delta}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-s_{0}+4\delta+(\frac{1}{2}-s_{0})\lor 0},

while in the case
N_{2}\gg N_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{X0mult}\lesssim\frac{N^{s}N_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{(N\vee N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}N_{\max}^{s+s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\lesssim(N\vee N_{3})^{s-s_{0}+2\delta}N_{\max}^{-s-s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-2s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}+4\delta}.

In both cases, we have a negative power of the largest dyadic, thus completing the estimate for (
4.13
).

Bound for (
4.14
):

We move onto the estimate for (
4.14
). By removing the outer modulation restriction and then using duality, it suffices to control

\displaystyle N^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}\bigg(\frac{\widehat{g}}{\langle\sigma\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\bigg)^{\vee}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}v\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}[\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}]dtdx

for
g\in L^{2}_{t,x}
of unit norm. Using Cauchy-Schwarz, (
3.6
), and Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle N^{s}N_{23}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}v\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\bigg\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\bigg(\frac{\widehat{g}}{\langle\sigma\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{s}N_{23}(N_{2}\wedge N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{s_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}\vee N_{3}}u_{j_{1}}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}\wedge N_{3}}u_{j_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.

These dyadic factors are controlled by (
4.18
), thus the proof follows from the previous case.

Bound for (
4.15
):

By duality, Hölder’s inequality, (
3.8
), and Bernstein’s inequality, we have

(
4.15
)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{23}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}[\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}g}\cdot\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v]\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}u_{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}(N_{2}N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{N_{1}^{s}K^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{X^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}u_{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}

We consider the ensuing multiplier which is

\displaystyle\frac{N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}(N_{2}N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{N_{1}^{s}K^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{(N\vee N_{3})^{s-s_{0}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}}}{N_{\max}^{s-10\delta}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-s_{0}+10\delta+(\frac{1}{2}-s_{0})\lor 0}

where we have used that
s+s_{0}>\frac{1}{2}+10\delta
and
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}+5\delta
. Under these conditions, we can then sum over all of the dyadics.

Bound for (
4.16
):

Recall that
N_{1}\sim N_{\max}
.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, to be chosen later.
By duality, Hölder’s inequality, (
3.8
), and Bernstein’s inequality, we control (
4.16
) by

\displaystyle N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}N_{1}^{-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{(2)}\|_{L^{2(1+\varepsilon)}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}N_{(3)}^{1-s_{0}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}N_{(2)}^{1-s_{0}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}{N_{1}^{s}K^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXxXXXxXXXXXXXXXXX}\times\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\|_{X^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}u_{(2)}\|_{X^{s_{0}-1,1}}.

We now consider the dyadic multiplier. We have

\displaystyle\frac{N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}N_{(3)}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}-s_{0}}N_{(2)}^{1-s_{0}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}{N_{1}^{s}K^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{s}N_{(2)}^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}+10\delta+\frac{2\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}N_{(3)}^{1-s_{0}+\varepsilon}}{N_{1}^{s}(N\lor N_{3})^{1-\varepsilon}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\alpha},

where for
N_{1}\sim N
we have
\alpha=-1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}+(\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}+10\delta+\frac{2\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)})\lor 0+(1-s_{0}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)})\lor 0
, and for
N_{1}\sim N_{(2)}\gg N
, we have
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}-s+10\delta+2\varepsilon+(-1+s+\varepsilon)\lor 0+(1-s_{0}+\varepsilon)\lor 0
, which gives
\alpha<0
in both cases, given
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small so that
2\varepsilon<\delta
and
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}+6\delta
.

Bound for (
4.17
):

We argue that due to impossible frequency interactions, we must actually have that

\displaystyle\eqref{Xres}=0.

(4.19)

If
N_{2}\wedge N_{3}=N_{3}
, then from the Case 3 condition, we have
N\gg N_{3}
and hence, from (
4.9
), we have
|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|\sim N_{23}N
. If instead
N_{2}\wedge N_{3}=N_{2}
, then from the hyperplane condition
\xi=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}
and (
4.4
) which implies
N_{1}\gtrsim N\gg N_{2}
, we have
|\xi-\xi_{3}|=|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}|\sim N_{1}\gtrsim(N\vee N_{3})
. Therefore, in either case, from (
4.9
), we have

\displaystyle K\lesssim|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|\leq|\sigma|+|\sigma_{1}|+|\sigma_{2}|+|\sigma_{3}|,

where
K
was defined in (
4.12
). However, due to the projectors
\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}
in (
4.17
), we have

\displaystyle|\sigma|+|\sigma_{1}|+|\sigma_{2}|+|\sigma_{3}|\ll K,

which is incompatible with the lower bound and hence (
4.19
) holds true.

This completes the proof of (
4.2
).
∎

We now give the main trilinear estimates for the case of (
2.16
); namely, without the Hardy space assumption. The key new ingredients relative to Proposition
4.1
are the decomposition in Section
3.4
and the
L^{p}
boundedness property in (
3.11
).
We specify a particular case for the parameters in Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
3.9
that we will use. We choose
s=s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}
, let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\delta\ll\tfrac{1}{100}(s_{0}-\tfrac{1}{4})

(4.20)

and take
0<\sigma<1
such that

\displaystyle\sigma=\sigma(\delta)=\tfrac{1}{4}+11\delta.

(4.21)

Of course, from (
3.49
), we could take any
\sigma>\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}+10\delta
but taking the worst possible regularity in (
4.21
) is sufficient.

Proposition 4.2

(Trilinear estimates for INLS (
2.16
))
.

Let
s\geq s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}
and
0<T\leq 1
. Let
\delta>0
and
\sigma=\sigma(\delta)
be as in (
4.21
).
Then, for any
u_{j}\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s_{0},4}_{x}
and

\displaystyle u_{j}\in X_{T}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}},\quad u_{j}-\mathcal{I}[B(u_{j},w_{j})]\in X^{s_{0}-1,1}_{T},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{I}[B(u_{j},w_{j})]\in X^{s_{0}-\sigma,\frac{1}{2}}_{T},

for
j=2,3
,
with

\displaystyle Y(u_{2},u_{3}):=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=2}^{3}\big(\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u_{j}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+\|u_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|u_{j}\|_{X_{T}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{2,3\}\\
j_{1}\neq j_{2}\end{subarray}}\Big(\|u_{j_{1}}\|_{X_{T}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}}\|u_{j_{2}}-\mathcal{I}[B(u_{j_{2}},w_{j_{2}})]\|_{X_{T}^{s_{0}-1,1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\|u_{j_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\mathcal{I}[B(u_{j_{2}},w_{j_{2}})]\|_{X_{T}^{s_{0}-\sigma,\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)

and for any
v,w\in X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}
satisfying
v=\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}v
and
w=\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}w
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[v\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}Y(u_{2},u_{3}),

(4.22)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}[w\,\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta}\|w\|_{X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}Y(u_{2},u_{3}).

(4.23)

Proof.

Fix parameters
(s,s_{0},\delta,\sigma)
as in the statement.
We will only show (
4.22
), as the estimate for (
4.23
) follows by the same argument. In particular, note that for both quantities, the projections guarantee the following relation between the frequencies
|\xi_{1}|\gtrsim|\xi|\lor|\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}|
, as used in (
4.4
) in the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
By further examining this proof,
we see that we only need to modify the argument when one of the functions
u_{2},u_{3}
is placed in
X^{s_{0}-1,1}_{T}
.
In particular,
it remains to estimate the term (
4.16
), since in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, we placed the term
\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{(2)}
into the space
L^{2(1+\varepsilon)}_{t,x}
which was controlled in
X^{s_{0}-1,1}
. We need to refine this step as we no longer can assume that
u_{(2)}
lives in
X^{s_{0}-1,1}
.

Going one step back in the computation for (
4.16
), by duality, Cauchy-Schwarz and (
3.8
), we have to control

\displaystyle N^{s}N^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}_{23}N_{1}^{-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.

(4.24)

We focus on the last factor, using the triangle inequality to bound it as follows

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\{u_{(2)}-\mathcal{I}[B(u_{(2)},w_{(2)})]\}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathcal{I}[B(u_{(2)},w_{(2)})]\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.

(4.26)

For the contribution to (
4.24
) coming from (
4.25
), we can argue exactly as we did for (
4.16
) and we have that this contribution is bounded by

\displaystyle N_{\max}^{0-}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{X^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\{u_{(2)}-\mathcal{I}[B(u_{(2)},w_{(2)})]\}\|_{X^{s_{0}-1,1}},

thus allowing us to sum in all the dyadics.

We now consider the contribution to (
4.24
) coming from (
4.26
).
Let
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, to be chosen later.
In view of the definition of
K
in (
4.12
), (
4.11
), and the Case 3 assumption (
N_{(3)}\ll N\wedge N_{(2)}
) we have
K\gg N_{(3)}^{2}
.
Thus, by (
3.11
), interpolation, and Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{(3)}^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}-\frac{\varepsilon(1/2-s_{0})}{(1+\varepsilon)}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{X^{0,0}}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}.

(4.27)

Thus, by Hölder’s inequality and (
4.27
), we have

(
4.26
)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathcal{I}[B(u_{(2)},w_{(2)})]\|_{L^{2(1+\varepsilon)}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N_{(3)}^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}-\frac{\varepsilon(1/2-s_{0})}{(1+\varepsilon)}}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}N_{(2)}^{s_{0}-\sigma(s_{0},\delta)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}\|J^{s_{0}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(3)}}u_{(3)}\|_{X^{0,0}}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXxXXXXXXXXXXX}\times\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{(2)}}\mathcal{I}[B(u_{(2)},w_{(2)})]\|_{X^{s_{0}-\sigma,\frac{1}{2}}}.

Inserting this bound into (
4.24
), we obtain the dyadic factor

\displaystyle\frac{N^{s}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+10\delta}(N_{2}\wedge N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s_{0}-\frac{\varepsilon(1/2-s_{0})}{(1+\varepsilon)}}}{N_{1}^{s}K^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{s_{0}-\sigma(s_{0},\delta)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(N\vee N_{(3)})^{s-2s_{0}+\sigma+\frac{\varepsilon(s_{0}+1/2)}{1+\varepsilon}}}{N_{1}^{s-10\delta-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{\max}^{-2s_{0}+\sigma+10\delta+\frac{\varepsilon(s_{0}+3/2)}{1+\varepsilon}}.

This is a negative power of
N_{\max}
provided that

\displaystyle s_{0}+(s_{0}-\sigma)>10\delta+\tfrac{\varepsilon(s_{0}+3/2)}{1+\varepsilon}.

This is clearly satisfied for since
s_{0}>\frac{1}{4}
and by taking
\delta,\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small.
This completes the proof of (
4.22
). ∎

We now control the remaining contributions from the right-hand sides of (
3.14
) and (
3.15
)

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
0<T\leq 1
,
\frac{1}{4}<s<\frac{2}{3}
,
0<\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{8}
, and
u\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}[

\displaystyle e^{i\beta F}u\mathcal{L}_{h}(|u|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\max_{p\in\{2,3,4\}}\big(\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}\big)\big(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{4}+}_{x}}^{3}\big)\|J^{s}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|J^{\frac{1}{4}+}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}^{2},

(4.28)

for
\mathcal{Q}_{h}
as in (
2.15
).

Proof.

From Lemma
3.1
, Cauchy-Schwarz, Sobolev embedding, and the assumption
\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{8}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}f\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}-\delta_{0}}\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}f\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-2\delta_{0}}}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}-\delta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

We then use (
2.10
), Bernstein’s inequality, and the fractional Leibniz rule. We give the bounds for (
4.28
):

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll 1}(u\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|u|^{2}))\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{\text{op}}\|u\|_{L^{4}_{x}}^{3},

\displaystyle\|u\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{3}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{L^{3}\to L^{3}}\|u\|_{L^{6}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{L^{3}\to L^{3}}\|J^{\frac{1}{4}+}u\|_{L^{4}_{x}}^{3},

\displaystyle\|D^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim 1}(u\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|u|^{2}))\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}\|D^{s}u\|_{L_{x}^{4}}\|J^{\frac{1}{4}+}u\|_{L^{4}_{x}}^{2}.

This completes the proof. ∎

It is clear that by using (
2.11
) we also have corresponding difference estimates for (
4.28
).

5.
Well-posedness and the infinite depth limit

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.1
. As the general argument here is quite standard we will be brief.
For further details, we refer to
[
10
, Sections 5-6]
where similar arguments were made for (
2.16
) with periodic boundary conditions; the argument itself is based on that in
[
47
,
48
,
25
]
. We will focus on the case of (
2.16
) with the same result for (
1.8
) with the Hardy space assumption following in a similar but simpler fashion.

By the result of
[
17
]
, we have local-in-time well-posedness for (
2.16
) in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
for any
s>\frac{1}{2}
. We fix
s_{1}>\frac{1}{2}
but close to
\frac{1}{2}
and fix
\frac{1}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{1}{2}<s_{1}
. As we do not know if the solutions in
H^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R})
are global-in-time, we first obtain apriori estimates on these solutions. These will ensure that their maximal time of existence is lower bounded by a time
T_{\ast}>0
only depending on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}
and not on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{1}}}
.

Given
u_{0}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, let
u\in C([0,T_{\max}];H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))
be the solution to (
2.16
). We then define

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{T}^{s}(u)=\max\bigg(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},&\|J^{s}u\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}},\|v(0)\|_{H^{s}},\|w(0)\|_{H^{s}},\\
&\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{v}(u)\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}},\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{w}(u)\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}\bigg),\end{split}

(5.1)

where
v,w
are the gauged variables (
3.13
),

\mathcal{N}_{{v}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{w}
are the nonlinear terms defined in (
3.14
) and (
3.15
), and
0<\delta_{0}\ll 1
is fixed as in (
4.20
). Given
0<h\leq\infty
, we also set

\displaystyle L_{h}:=\max_{p\in\{2,4\}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}

with the understanding that
L_{\infty}=|\gamma|
.

First, from the Duhamel formula for the
v
and
w
equations in (
3.14
)-(
3.15
), together with Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|v\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T}}+\|w\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\big(\|v(0)\|_{H^{s}}+\|w(0)\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{v}(u)\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{w}(u)\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}N^{s}_{T}(u).

By combining Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
3.9
, Lemma
3.10
, (
3.14
)-(
3.15
), Proposition
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}N^{\sigma}_{T}(u)&\leq C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})^{a_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma}}\\
&+C_{2}\big\{(T^{\theta_{1}}(1+L_{h})+M^{-\theta_{2}})Q_{2}(N_{T}^{s_{0}}(u))+T^{\theta_{3}}M(1+L_{h})Q_{3}(N_{T}^{s_{0}}(u))\big\}N_{T}^{\sigma}(u),\end{split}

(5.2)

where
\sigma\in\{s_{0},s,s_{1}\}
, and
for some constants
a_{1},\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3}>0
and
C_{1},C_{2}>0
and where
Q_{2},Q_{3}
are some non-negative polynomials, where
Q_{3}
has no constant term.

Whilst the constants depend on
\{s_{0},s,s_{1}\}
, we only use these three regularities and can thus take the maximum of the given constants over
\{s_{0},s,s_{1}\}
.
We first put
\sigma=s_{0}
in (
5.2
).
We then choose
M\gg 1
depending on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle C_{2}M^{-\theta_{2}}Q_{2}(4R)<\tfrac{1}{4}\quad\text{where}\quad R:=C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})^{a_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}.

(5.3)

Then, given this choice of
M
, we choose
T_{\ast}=T_{\ast}(M,L_{h})>0

so that

\displaystyle C_{2}(1+L_{h})\big(T_{*}^{\theta_{1}}Q_{2}(4R)+T_{*}^{\theta_{3}}MQ_{3}(4R)\big)<\tfrac{1}{4}.

(5.4)

This verifies that

N_{T}^{s_{0}}(u)\leq 2R
, which is the apriori bound at regularity
\sigma=s_{0}
. By using this information in (
1.20
) at regularity
\sigma=s_{1}
and reducing
T=T(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})>0
if necessary, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T^{\prime}}H^{s_{1}}}\leq N_{T^{\prime}}^{s_{1}}(u)\leq 2C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})^{a_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{1}}},

(5.5)

for any
0<T^{\prime}\leq T

which implies that the maximal time of existence for these solutions is bounded from below by
T_{\ast}=T_{\ast}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})
.
Note that
T_{\ast}
also depends on
L_{h}
but can be chosen uniformly in
h
only depending on
\sup_{1\leq h\leq\infty}L_{h}
in view of (
2.14
).

As for the uniqueness and continuity of the flow map, we consider differences of
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
solutions and derive a difference estimate. Given two such solutions
u_{1},u_{2}
to (
2.16
) with initial data
u_{j}(0)\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, we consider the difference
U:=u_{1}-u_{2}
and
W:=w_{1}-w_{2}
, where
w_{j}:=\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}u_{j}
.
We also define the primitives
F_{j}=F_{j}[u_{j}]
as in (
2.5
) and the attendant gauged variables
v_{j}=v_{j}[u_{j}]
as in (
3.13
) with corresponding difference
V:=v_{1}-v_{2}
.
By the previous analysis, we have control on
N_{T}^{\sigma}(u_{j})
,
j=1,2
, for
\sigma\in\{s_{0},s\}
and for time
0<T\leq T_{\ast}
. In particular, by the Duhamel formulas for
v_{j},w_{j}
in (
3.14
)-(
3.15
), this provides control on the
X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T}
norms of
v_{j},w_{j}
.
We then obtain difference estimates for the norms appearing in (
5.1
), where we additionally use Lemma
3.11
.
We note that unlike the situation for the Benjamin-Ono equation in
[
47
]
, the difference estimate does not assume that the data
u_{j}(0)
,
j=1,2
, agree on low frequencies. Indeed, by Cauchy-Schwarz and (
2.5
) we see that

\displaystyle\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\lesssim\||u_{1}|^{2}-|u_{2}|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{1}_{x}}\lesssim(\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}+\|u_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}})\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}.

At the end of this procedure, we obtain

\displaystyle\|J^{\sigma}U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}\cap\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|V\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T}}+\|W\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T}}\leq C_{5}(\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s_{0}}},\|u_{2}(0)\|_{H^{s_{0}}})\|U(0)\|_{H^{\sigma}},

(5.6)

for any
0<T\leq T_{0}
and
\sigma\in\{s_{0},s\}
.

For the existence of solutions in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
, we fix
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
and consider the sequence of approximations
u_{0,j}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\{\mathbf{1}_{[-j,j]}\widehat{u}_{0}\}
with corresponding
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
solutions
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
. By the previous results, these all belong to
C([0,T_{\ast}];H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))
, where
T_{\ast}=T_{\ast}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})>0
.
Since
u_{0,j}
converges to
u_{0}
in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
, we may choose
M
in (
5.3
) uniformly in
j\in\mathbb{N}
and moreover, the sequence is equicontinous in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
and thus uniformly tight on the Fourier side. This property guarantees that we may choose
M
uniformly in
j\in\mathbb{N}
in order to obtain smallness for the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.66
). By (
5.6
), we see that the sequence
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is then Cauchy in the norm appearing in
N_{T}^{s}
and hence converges to a limit
u
there, which satisfies (
2.16
) in the distributional sense and has
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
. This completes the proof of the local well-posedness in Theorem
1.1
.

Finally, we consider the infinite depth limit as
h\to\infty
.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We only give sketch of the argument, and refer to similar full details in
[
10
]
. See also
[
12
]
.
Given
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
and a net
\{u_{0,h}\}_{1\leq h<\infty}
in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
with
u_{0,h}\to u_{0}
in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R})
, we denote by
u_{h}
and
u_{\infty}
the global solutions to (
2.16
) and (
1.8
), respectively, with
u_{h}|_{t=0}=u_{0,h}
and
u_{\infty}|_{t=0}=u_{0}
, constructed in Theorem
1.2
. Moreover, for
1\leq h\leq\infty
, we write
F_{h}=F_{h}[u_{h}]
for the primitives as in (
2.5
),
w_{h}
and
v_{h}
for the gauged variables in (
3.13
), and
U_{h}=u_{h}-u_{\infty}
,
V_{h}=v_{h}-v_{\infty}
, and
W_{h}=w_{h}-w_{\infty}
.
Repeating the process for the apriori bounds in (
5.5
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq h\leq\infty}N^{s}_{T_{*}}(u_{h})\leq 2C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})^{a_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}},

where
T_{*}=T_{*}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})>0
as in (
5.4
), and where this choice can be made uniformly in
h
.
Then, by repeating the process to obtain the difference estimates in (
5.6
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|J^{s}U_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{*}}L^{2}_{x}\cap\widetilde{L^{4}_{T_{*},x}}}+\|V_{h}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T_{*}}}+\|W_{h}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}_{T_{*}}}\\
&\leq C_{2}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})\big(\|u_{0}-u_{0,h}\|_{H^{s}}+\max_{p\in\{2,4\}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}-\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}\big),\end{split}

(5.7)

for some constant
C_{2}=C_{2}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})>0
depending only on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}
, uniform in
1\leq h<\infty
, and
where
\mathcal{Q}_{h}
is as in (
2.15
), and we recall that
\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}=-i\beta\mathcal{H}\partial_{x}+i\gamma\textup{Id}
.
Thus, from the convergence of the initial data, the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{h}-\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}=-i\beta\mathcal{G}_{h}
, where
\mathcal{G}_{h}
is as in (
1.21
), and (
2.14
),
we conclude that
u_{h}
solving (
2.16
) converges to the solution
u_{\infty}
to (
1.8
) in
C([0,T_{*}];H^{s}(\mathbb{R}))
as
h\to\infty
. Given a target time
T\gg 1
, since (
5.7
) for
h\gg 1
guarantees that
\|U_{h}(T_{*})\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}
, we can iterate the convergence argument to obtain convergence over the full interval
[0,T]
.
∎

6.
Conservation laws

In this section, we state our new Lax pair and use it to obtain low-regularity apriori bounds for (
1.1
).
Given
0<h\leq\infty
, we define

\displaystyle\Pi_{+,h}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+i\mathcal{T}_{h})

(6.1)

with
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}=\mathcal{H}
. Indeed, when
h=\infty
, (
1.6
) implies that
\Pi_{+,\infty}=\mathbf{P}_{+}
.
For finite
h>0
,
\Pi_{+,h}
has a singularity at the zero frequency that is even more severe.
With (
6.1
), we can also write (
1.1
) (with
\gamma=0
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+i\partial_{x}^{2}u=2\beta u\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}),

(6.2)

where
\beta\in\{\pm 1\}
. We recall that
\beta=1
corresponds to the defocusing case and
\beta=-1
to the focusing, and again encompassing CCM (
1.8
) when
h=\infty
.
We now state the new Lax pair for (
6.2
) for all
0<h\leq\infty
.

Proposition 6.1

(Lax pair)
.

For any
0<h\leq\infty
,
u(t)
solves (
6.2
) on the line if and only if the operators

\mathcal{L}_{u;h}=-i\partial_{x}+\beta u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{P}_{u;h}=-i\partial_{x}^{2}+2\beta u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}

(6.3)

on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfy

\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_{u;h}=[\mathcal{P}_{u;h},\mathcal{L}_{u;h}].

(6.4)

When
h=\infty
, this resembles the well-known Lax pair for CCM in the literature for the special case
u\in L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R})
, while for
h<\infty
this appears to be new.
Indeed, the Lax operator in
[
52
]
is a
2\times 2
operator-valued matrix, similar to that of NLS.

Proof.

Let
f\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a test function. Let
\beta=\mp 1
denote the sign of the nonlinearity in (
6.2
).
We compute

\displaystyle[\mathcal{P}_{u;h},\mathcal{L}_{u;h}]f

\displaystyle=-i\beta\partial_{x}^{2}[u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]-2i\beta u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime})+2u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}[|u|^{2}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+2\beta i\partial_{x}[u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]+i\beta u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime\prime})-2u\Pi_{+,h}(|u|^{2}\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)).

Consider first the terms which we are quadratic in
u
. By developing them further, we have

\displaystyle{-i}\beta\partial_{x}^{2}[u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]-2i\beta u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime})+2\beta i\partial_{x}[u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]+i\beta u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=-i\beta\big\{\partial_{x}^{2}[u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]+2u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime})-2\partial_{x}[u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]-u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=-i\beta\big\{(\partial_{x}^{2}u)\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)+2(\partial_{x}u)\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)+u\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}^{2}(\overline{u}f)+2u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXX}-2(\partial_{x}u)\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)-2u\partial_{x}^{2}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)-u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=-i\beta\big\{(\partial_{x}^{2}u)\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)-u\partial_{x}^{2}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)+2u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime})-u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f^{\prime\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=-i\beta\big\{(\partial_{x}^{2}u)\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)-u\Pi_{+,h}((\partial_{x}^{2}\overline{u})f)\big\}.

(6.5)

Now focus on the quartic in
u
terms in the commutator:

\displaystyle 2u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}[|u|^{2}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]-2u\Pi_{+,h}[|u|^{2}\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]=2u\Pi_{+,h}[\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)].

We claim that

\displaystyle\begin{split}2u\Pi_{+,h}[\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)]=2u\big[\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\cdot\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)+\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})}\cdot\overline{u}f)\big],\end{split}

(6.6)

whereupon, combining with (
6.5
), we will have shown that

\displaystyle[\mathcal{P}_{u;h},\mathcal{L}_{u;h}]=\beta(-i\partial_{x}^{2}u+2\beta u\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}))\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}+\beta u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{(-i\partial_{x}^{2}u+2\beta u\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}))}

which completes the proof.

We expand the left-hand side of (
6.6
) using (
6.1
):

LHS
(
6.6
)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}u(1+i\mathcal{T}_{h})(\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}))(1+i\mathcal{T}_{h})(\overline{u}f)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}u\Big\{(\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}))\overline{u}f+i\big[\mathcal{T}_{h}((\partial_{x}|u|^{2})\overline{u}f)+\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\mathcal{T}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\big]-\mathcal{T}_{h}[\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\mathcal{T}_{h}(\overline{u}f)]\Big\}.

Similarly, for the right-hand side of (
6.6
), using (
6.1
) we have:

RHS
(
6.6
)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}u\Big\{2\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\overline{u}f+i\{\mathcal{T}_{h}(\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\overline{u}f)+\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\mathcal{T}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXX}+\mathcal{T}_{h}[\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\cdot\overline{u}f]-\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\cdot\mathcal{T}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\Big\}.

Taking the difference of these, we find:

\displaystyle\text{RHS}\eqref{cubic}-\text{LHS}\eqref{cubic}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}u\Big\{\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\overline{u}f+\mathcal{T}_{h}[\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\cdot\overline{u}f]-\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\cdot\mathcal{T}_{h}(\overline{u}f)+\mathcal{T}_{h}[\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\mathcal{T}_{h}(\overline{u}f)]\Big\}.

Using (
2.12
) and noting
M_{\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})}=0
, we see that

\displaystyle\text{RHS}\eqref{cubic}-\text{LHS}\eqref{cubic}=0,

hence verifying (
6.6
).
∎

Our above verification of (
6.4
) hinged on the Cotlar-type identity (
2.12
). It is conceivable that (a slightly modified version of) the Lax pair (
6.3
) would also work in the periodic case. However, (
2.12
) requires modifications due to the presence of the zero frequency, which cannot be avoided by imposing a mean-zero constraint:
\int_{\mathbb{T}}udx
is not a conservation law for (
1.1
). Consequently, we were not able at this point to give a Lax pair for the periodic (
1.1
) for finite
h
.

In the next result, we make sense of the Lax operator
\mathcal{L}_{u;h}
as a self-adjoint operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. To this end, we need to assume that
u\in H^{\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{R})
. For the case of CCM (
1.8
) in
L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R})
, this restriction on the potentials can be weakened to
u\in L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proposition 6.2

(Lax operator)
.

Fix
0<h\leq\infty
. Given
u\in H^{\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{R})
, the operator

\mathcal{L}_{u;h}f=-i\partial_{x}f+\beta u\Pi_{+,h}(\overline{u}f)

with domain
H^{1}
is self-adjoint. Moreover, there is a constant
C\geq 1
so that for
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\kappa\geq C\big(1+\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\big)^{4},

(6.7)

we have

\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\leq\tfrac{3}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

(6.8)

as quadratic forms, where
\mathcal{L}_{0}:=\mathcal{L}_{0;h}=-i\partial_{x}
.

Proof.

For
\kappa>0
, let
R_{0}(\kappa)=(|\partial_{x}|+\kappa)^{-1}.

We write

\Pi_{+,h}f=\tfrac{1}{2}i\mathcal{J}_{h}f+\tfrac{1}{2}(1+i\mathcal{K}_{h})f,

(6.9)

where
\mathcal{K}_{h}
is defined as in (
2.13
) and
\mathcal{J}_{h}
is the integral operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{h}f(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2h}\text{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textup{sgn}\bigg(\frac{\pi(x-y)}{2h}\bigg)f(y)dy.

(6.10)

Using Sobolev embedding and Lemma
2.3
, this yields

\|u(1+i\mathcal{K}_{h})\overline{u}R_{0}f\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\big(1+\|\mathcal{K}_{h}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}\big)\|R_{0}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.11)

For the contribution of
\mathcal{J}_{h}
, we have

\|\mathcal{J}_{h}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|f\|_{L^{1}},

(6.12)

which again is uniform in
1\leq h<\infty
,
and so

\|u\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}R_{0}f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|R_{0}f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h\kappa}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.13)

Collecting the previous two steps, we see that we may choose
\kappa\geq 1
sufficiently large, as in (
6.7
), so that

\|u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}g\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}R_{0}\|_{\textup{op}}\|(|\partial_{x}|+\kappa)g\|_{L^{2}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\|\mathcal{L}_{0}g\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa\|g\|_{L^{2}}\big).

Self-adjointness then follows from the Kato–Rellich Theorem (see Th. X.12 in
[
54
]
).

From (
6.11
) and (
6.13
), we also see that there is a choice of
C\geq 1
so that (
6.7
) ensures

\|u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}R_{0}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq\tfrac{1}{10}.

This in turn guarantees that

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle f,(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})f\big\rangle-\big\langle f,(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})f\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}f\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathcal{L}_{0}f\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{5}\|(|\partial_{x}|+\kappa)f\|_{L^{2}}\||\partial_{x}|f\|_{L^{2}}+\tfrac{1}{100}\|(|\partial_{x}|+\kappa)f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{21}{100}\|(|\partial_{x}|+\kappa)f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{21}{100}\langle f,(|\partial_{x}|+\kappa)^{2}f\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{21}{50}\langle f,(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})f\rangle.

The claim (
6.8
) then follows.
∎

We note that the Peter operator
\mathcal{P}_{u;h}
in (
6.3
) is special because (
6.2
) may be written as

\partial_{t}u=\mathcal{P}_{u;h}u.

(6.14)

Combining this with (
6.4
), it is straightforward to verify that the “polynomial” quantities

\displaystyle E_{k}^{h}(u)=\langle u,\mathcal{L}^{k}_{u;h}u\rangle\quad\text{for}\quad u\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

(6.15)

are conserved for any
k\geq 0
. Moreover, these functionals extend continuously to
H^{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbb{R})
; see Proposition
6.4
below for details.

More generally, the following lemma implies that for arbitrary functions
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, the quantity

t\mapsto\big\langle u(t),f(\mathcal{L}_{u(t);h})\,u(t)\big\rangle

is automatically conserved. As we will discuss further below, by selecting a function
f
that is not a polynomial we will be able to address the intermediate regularities
s\in(\frac{1}{4},1]
in Theorem
1.2
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
u(t)
be a global
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
solution of (
6.2
). For any
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and any
\psi_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, there exists a unique
C_{t}L^{2}\cap C^{1}_{t}H^{-2}
solution to the initial value problem

\tfrac{d}{dt}\psi(t)=\mathcal{P}_{u(t);h}\psi(t)\quad\text{with}\quad\psi(t_{0})=\psi_{0}

(6.16)

and it is global in time. Moreover, for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
the mapping
\mathcal{U}(t;t_{0}):\psi_{0}\to\psi(t)
is unitary on
L^{2}
,

u(t)=\mathcal{U}(t;t_{0})u(t_{0}),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{L}_{u(t);h}=\mathcal{U}(t;t_{0})\mathcal{L}_{u(t_{0});h}\mathcal{U}^{*}(t;t_{0}).

(6.17)

Finally, if
\psi_{0}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, then so too is
\psi(t)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

The proof of this lemma is elementary. Indeed, the existence and uniqueness of solutions to (
6.16
) follows from a Bona–Smith regularization and a contraction mapping argument. As
\mathcal{P}_{u;h}
is anti-selfadjoint, we deduce that the
L^{2}
norm is conserved; this allows us to extend solutions of (
6.16
) globally in time, and demonstrates that
\mathcal{U}(t;t_{0})
is unitary. A standard persistence of regularity argument then shows that
\psi(t)
is as smooth as
\psi_{0}
is. Lastly, the two identities in (
6.17
) follow from the property (
6.14
) satisfied by
\mathcal{P}_{u;h}
and the Lax pair relation (
6.4
). We refer to
[
30
, Prop. 2.3]
for the full details in the case of CCM.

We are now equipped to prove our a-priori estimates:

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The main point is to establish (
1.20
). Then, by iterating Theorem
1.1
, we obtain the global well-posedness of solutions to (
1.1
) with small
L^{2}
-norm initial data.
Consider the quantity

F(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2}):=\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u\big\rangle.

By Loewner’s Theorem, the function
x\mapsto x^{\frac{1}{4}}
on
(0,\infty)
is operator monotone (see
[
55
]
for details). In particular, the relation (
6.8
) implies

F\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\big)\leq F(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq F\big(\tfrac{3}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\big),

and so

\tfrac{1}{C}F(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq F(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq CF(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

for some constant
C>0
. On the other hand, from (
6.17
) we see that

\big\langle u(t),(\mathcal{L}_{u(t);h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u(t)\big\rangle=\big\langle u(0),(\mathcal{L}_{u(0);h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u(0)\big\rangle.

Combing the previous two steps, we find

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\langle u(t),(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u(t)\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\big\langle u(t),(\mathcal{L}_{u(t);h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u(t)\big\rangle

\displaystyle=C\big\langle u(0),(\mathcal{L}_{u(0);h}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u(0)\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\big\langle u(0),(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}u(0)\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}+C^{2}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(6.18)

Now we use a bootstrap argument. Given
u(0)
, consider

\kappa=C\big(1+2C\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\big)^{4}.

Here,
C\geq 1
is larger than both the constant from the previous paragraph and the constant from (
6.7
). For this
\kappa
and any time interval
[0,T]
on which

\|u(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\leq 2C\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}},

(6.19)

we know that the monotonicity relation (
6.8
) holds. Then, (
6.18
) demonstrates that

\|u(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}\leq C^{2}\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}+C^{\frac{5}{2}}\big(1+2C\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\big)^{2}\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Taking
\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\ll C^{-\frac{5}{2}}
, we deduce

\|u(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}\leq\tfrac{3}{2}C\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

(6.20)

Comparing this with (
6.19
), we conclude that (
6.20
) holds for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. This proves the
s=\frac{1}{4}
case of (
1.20
).

Now that we know

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\|u(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}<\infty,

we may fix
\kappa
sufficiently large so that the monotonicity relation (
6.8
) holds for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. An argument parallel to (
6.18
) using the quantity

F_{s}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})=\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s}u\big\rangle

then demonstrates that (
1.20
) holds for any
\frac{1}{4}<s\leq 1
. Note that by Loewner’s Theorem, the restriction
s\leq 1
is necessary for the function
x\mapsto x^{s}
to be operator monotone.
∎

Lastly, we establish the convergence of the polynomial conservation laws for INLS (
1.1
) (with
\gamma=0
) to the polynomial conservation laws of CCM (
1.8
).

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
0<h\leq\infty
,
s\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, and
u\in W^{\max(s,0),4}(\mathbb{R})
. Then,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}\|_{H^{s+1}(\mathbb{R})\to H^{s}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|u\|_{W^{\max(s,0),4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

(6.21)

Furthermore, there exists
\theta=\theta(s)>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{u;h}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}\|_{H^{s+1}(\mathbb{R})\to H^{s}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-\theta}(\|u\|_{W^{\max(s,0),4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{\max(s,0)}}^{2}).

(6.22)

with implicit constant uniform over
1\leq h\leq\infty
.
Moreover, if
E_{k}^{h}(u)
,
k\geq 0
, is the generalised energy in (
6.15
), it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\to\infty}E_{k}^{h}(u)=E_{k}^{\infty}(u)

(6.23)

for any
u\in H^{\frac{k}{2}}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

We first establish (
6.23
) assuming (
6.21
) and (
6.22
). Note that (
6.21
) and (
6.22
) imply

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq h\leq\infty}\|\mathcal{L}_{u;h}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}\lesssim 1+\|u\|_{W^{\max(s,0),4}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{\max(s,0)}}^{2}.

for any
s\geq-\frac{1}{2}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq h\leq\infty}\|\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{j}u\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim(1+\|u\|_{W^{s+j-1,4}}^{2j}+\|u\|_{H^{s+j-1}}^{2j})\|u\|_{H^{s+j}}

(6.24)

for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
s\geq-\frac{1}{2}
.
By a direct computation, we see that
E_{j}^{h}(u)=E_{j}^{\infty}(u)
for
j=0,1
. Indeed,
E_{0}^{h}(u)=\int|u|^{2}dx
and
E_{1}^{h}(u)
is the momentum in (
1.11
).
Thus, to prove (
6.23
), it suffices to consider
k\geq 2
. Consider first the case when
k\in 2\mathbb{N}
. Then

\displaystyle E_{k}^{h}(u)=\langle\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{k/2}u,\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{k/2}u\rangle,

so that by (
6.24
),

\displaystyle|E_{k}^{h}(u)-E_{k}^{\infty}(u)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{k/2}u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{k/2}u\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\|(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{k/2}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{k/2})u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h^{\theta}}(1+\|u\|_{H^{k/2}})^{2k}\|u\|_{H^{k/2}}^{2}\to 0

as
h\to\infty
.

Now consider the case when
k\in 2\mathbb{N}+1
so that we may write
k=2\ell+1
for some
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|E_{k}^{h}(u)-E_{k}^{\infty}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\langle\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}u,\mathcal{L}_{u;h}\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}u\rangle-\langle\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell}u,\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell}u\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\langle(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell})u,\mathcal{L}_{u;h}\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}u\rangle\big|+\big|\langle\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell}u,(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty})\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}u\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+\big|\langle\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell}u,\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell})u\rangle\big|.

We just consider the first term since similar estimates will control the remaining two terms. By (
6.24
) and (
6.22
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell})u,\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell+1}u\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{\ell})u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{\ell+1}u\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h^{\theta}}(1+\|u\|_{H^{\ell+\frac{1}{2}}})^{2k}\|u\|_{H^{\ell+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{h^{\theta}}(1+\|u\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}})^{2k}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{k}{2}}}^{2}\to 0,

as
h\to\infty
. Repeating the argument for the remaining terms, we obtain (
6.23
) for
k\in 2\mathbb{N}+1
and thus for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

It remains to establish (
6.21
) and (
6.22
). We begin with (
6.21
). By (
6.3
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}f\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|f\|_{H^{s+1}}+\|u\mathbf{P}_{+}(\overline{u}f)\|_{H^{s}}.

It thus suffices to estimate the second term on the right-hand side above.
If
s=0
, we already controlled this term for
f\in H^{1}
in the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
If
s<0
, we use duality: with
\sigma=-s
so
0<\sigma\leq\frac{1}{2}
, by Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|u\mathbf{P}_{+}(\overline{u}f)\|_{H^{s}}&=\sup_{\|g\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1}\bigg|\int\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{-\sigma}g\cdot u\mathbf{P}_{+}(\overline{u}f)dx\bigg|\\
&\leq\sup_{\|g\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1}\|\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{-\sigma}g\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-2\sigma}}}\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|uf\|_{L^{\frac{4}{1+4\sigma}}}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{\|g\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1}\|g\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}}\\
&\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.\end{split}

(6.25)

This proves (
6.21
) for
s<0
.

Now we consider the case when
s>0
. By the fractional Leibniz rule, the boundedness of
\mathbf{P}_{+}
on
L^{4}(\mathbb{R})
, and Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\|u\mathbf{P}_{+}(\overline{u}f)\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}\|\mathbf{P}_{+}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{4}}+\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|\mathbf{P}_{+}(\overline{u}f)\|_{W^{s,4}}\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{s+1}}.

(6.26)

This completes the proof of (
6.21
).

We now turn to (
6.22
).
Using (
6.3
), (
6.9
), and the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{u;h}-\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\|u(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|u\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}.

(6.27)

We consider the first term on the right-hand side of (
6.27
), which we split further as

\displaystyle\|u(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\leq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}+\|u(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\geq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}=:\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}.

When
s<0
, following the computation in (
6.25
) and using Bernstein’s inequality and Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|u(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\leq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}[\overline{u}f]\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{\frac{4}{1-4s}}}\lesssim h^{-\frac{3+4s}{8}}\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

If
s\geq 0
, additionally using the fractional Leibniz rule, we have

\displaystyle\|u(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\leq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}[\overline{u}f]\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}\|(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\leq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}[\overline{u}f]\|_{W^{s,4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}[\overline{u}f]\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-\frac{1}{8}}\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|f\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-\frac{1}{8}}\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{s+1}}.

Combining these estimates, we have shown that there exists
\theta>0
such that

\displaystyle\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt\lesssim h^{-\theta}(\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{s<0}+\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{s\geq 0}).

(6.28)

Now we consider
I I
, for which we claim that

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}\lesssim h^{-\theta}\|u\|_{W^{\max(s,0),4}}^{2}.

(6.29)

By following the computations in (
6.25
) and (
6.26
), it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\geq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}\lesssim h^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(6.30)

for any
\frac{4}{3}\leq p\leq 4
.
Let

\displaystyle m(\xi)=\big[{-i}[\coth(h\xi)-\textup{sgn}(h\xi)]+\tfrac{i}{h\xi}\big](1-\eta_{h^{-1/2}}(\xi))

which is the Fourier multiplier associated with
(\mathcal{K}_{h}-\mathcal{H})\mathbf{P}_{\geq\frac{1}{h^{1/2}}}

Then, we have
|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}m(\xi)|\lesssim h^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\xi|^{-\alpha}
for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
and so (
6.30
) follows from the Mikhlin-Hörmander theorem
[
23
, Theorem 6.2.7]
.

It remains to control the second term on the right-hand side of (
6.27
). We define

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}f:=\mathcal{J}_{h}f-\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}f.

(6.31)

The operator
\mathcal{J}_{h}
is a Fourier multiplier operator with multiplier
(ih\xi)^{-1}
, which is singular at the origin. For
F\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
\alpha>0
,
D^{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{h}F
is a tempered distribution in the sense of
[
5
, Definition 1.26]
and thus the inhomogeneous Littlewood-Paley decomposition converges in the sense of tempered distributions:

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\dot{\mathbf{P}_{k}}[D^{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{h}F]=D^{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{h}F.

Similar to (
2.7
), we then have

\displaystyle D^{\alpha}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}F=\sum_{k\leq 2}\dot{\mathbf{P}_{k}}[D^{\alpha}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}F]

and thus using (
6.10
) and arguing as in (
2.8
), we have

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{k<2}2^{k\alpha}\|m_{1,\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{J}_{h}F\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|F\|_{L^{1}}.

(6.32)

We now write

\displaystyle\|u\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}\leq\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}+\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}.

Consider first the contribution from
\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}
. If
s\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\overline{u}f\|_{L^{2-}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

which is sufficient. If
s>0
, then we use the fractional Leibniz rule as in (
6.26
) and simply note that
\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}\lesssim\frac{1}{h}
. This implies the bound

\displaystyle\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}^{2}.

Next, for the contribution from
\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}
, we also consider the cases
s\leq 0
and
s>0
. If
s\leq 0
, we use the physical side formula (
6.10
), the definition (
6.31
), Bernstein’s inequality, and (
6.12
), to obtain

\displaystyle\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big(\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(6.33)

If
s>0
, then by the fractional Leibniz rule, similar computations as in (
6.33
) and using (
6.32
), we have

\displaystyle\|D^{s}[u\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)]\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|D^{s}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|D^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathcal{J}_{h}(\overline{u}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}\|u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

Thus, we have shown that

\displaystyle\|u\mathcal{J}_{h}\overline{u}\|_{H^{s+1}\to H^{s}}\lesssim\tfrac{1}{h}(\|u\|_{W^{\max(s,0),4}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{\max(s,0)}}^{2}).

Combining this with (
6.27
), (
6.28
), and (
6.29
) then proves (
6.22
).
∎
